:REASON:       Second manager addendum (reviewer input; acknowledge by content): include MODEL-FACING tool definitions/catalogue, toolset listings and tool notices in the sink inventory. Masking a listing while a credential-bearing routable name still enters the model's tool metadata does not close the exposure. For a credential-bearing identifier in strict mode, prefer WITHHOLDING/REFUSING that tool entry with a fixed diagnostic (no credential text) over inventing a differently-callable masked name; keep real routing identities internal. Clearly separate SDK-controlled surfaces from unmodified tool-result payloads in docs: no blanket malicious-server exfiltration guarantee.

:REASON:       Round-4 whole-message redaction also masks the SDK-owned fixed metadata-credential reason marker for short accepted credentials (the unchanged 94-character control asserts that marker). May the choke point preserve explicitly SDK-branded fixed reason suffixes, while strictly redacting all assembled dynamic message text and rejecting source-forged markers? This keeps withholding semantics/controls unchanged; otherwise the literal fixed reason-code contract conflicts with whole-message replacement.

* TX 2026-10-27 Tue 04:47:07 run.answered TASK-V1DZF
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261027-theaspirationalagent-ea895358-bc21-4ef6-a716-6dcee0dce1f0
:TIME:         [2026-10-27 Tue 04:47:07]

:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-V1DZF
:REASON:       Yes, with this exact rule: SDK-owned FIXED reason codes/markers (compile-time constants such as metadata-credential, provider-threw, etc.) are not credential-derived and may be preserved. Implement it STRUCTURALLY, not by text pattern: the choke point redacts the dynamic/assembled part, and the SDK attaches the fixed reason as a separate typed field (or appends the constant after redaction from a code-side enum). Never preserve any marker-looking text that came from server/peer/provider data; a forged marker in dynamic text must be redacted like any other text. The unchanged 94-character control must still pass. Document the rule in the report (fixed constants vs reflected data) and add a test where the server sends text containing the marker string plus the credential.

:REASON:       Acceptance C(2) requests byte-identical WebSocket requests. Both native Bun and npm ws generate a fresh Sec-WebSocket-Key per handshake; Bun does not expose a deterministic RNG for it. :REASON:       Manager additions (acknowledge each by content): (1) Classify the npm update-notifier request by actual scheme (https vs http, from the acquisition logs) and phase (acquisition vs test execution) and which gates it could affect; the original evidence stays as is - judge validity, not retroactive clearance. (2) For R8 snapshot fields whose tests pass on the base (credentialRequired, token, accessProvider, command, transport, protocol): do a mutation check per field - remove that field's capture and show a test fails; any surviving mutant means that field's snapshot claim is unproven. (3) Say whether DNS failure / connection refused / TLS failure / timeout / server refusal are all mapped to credential-unusable and whether that mislabels non-credential failures (report only; a fifth code is an owner question). (4) Note the limits: engine-unsupported tests simulate versions; isolation is an LD_PRELOAD loopback guard, not a namespace. (5) Provenance: private prior commits arrived only at finalize (QY3F8), so 'no reuse' was incidental - record it.

:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-EW3VV
:REASON:       One lifecycle edge needs a precise interpretation of your strict-only hook condition. If an operator changes credentialRequired from true to false before stop/removal, checking only the current flag skips disposal of that configuration’s previously created strict registry. Re-enabling the flag on the still-held object after stop could then resurrect old union entries. May cleanup instead be guarded by the existence of this framework’s strict lifetime record (created only when credentialRequired was true), with an immediate no-op for every never-strict configuration? This changes no non-strict registration, event order, caller object or output; it only disposes previously strict private state. The same three files and under-300-line ceiling remain satisfied. I will add a flag-false-before-stop / flag-true-after-stop lifetime control.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M5ZV61MEYAMQEGWE3N8CTWYV
:ASK_ID:       ask-7e4c24854e104a628939e613b410efef

Guard cleanup by the existence of this framework's strict lifetime record (created only when credentialRequired was true), immediate no-op for never-strict configurations; add the flag-false-before-stop / flag-true-after-stop control (no resurrection of old union entries). Also apply a peer-review caution to all writer paths: 'before the sink's final escaping' is not 'before all escaping' - a caller may already have JSON/unicode-escaped the credential. Keep the declared matching forms (raw, percent/form either hex case, JSON-escaped, slash-escaped JSON, unicode-escaped, Authorization header form) at the boundary and include representative already-escaped witnesses at each distinct writer path; do not reduce matching to a literal substring replacement. A deliberate template-collision test stays required. Quote back in one sentence.

* TX 2026-10-28 Wed 00:50:16 run.asked TASK-EW3VV
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261028-theaspirationalagent-b038f86e-6322-43fb-ac9a-093bfa021316
:TIME:         [2026-10-28 Wed 00:50:16]
:TYPE:         run.asked
:ACTOR:        orgasmic
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-EW3VV
:REASON:       A final real-route probe found a lifetime gap: register a configuration as non-strict, mutate its original object to credentialRequired=true, then perform a real strict dial on that same object. Both engines accept the credential but neither exporting sink sees it, because the framework never created an owner registry; the dial has only its standalone registry. All static/mixed/removal scenarios remain green. Does runtime enabling require union adoption here? I propose allowing lifecycle bookkeeping at registration for all held configs (an inert ownership record, no wrapping/listeners/output changes for legacy), creating an accepted set only upon a strict dial, and deleting the ownership record on removal/stop/final close. This stays in the same three files and below 300 net lines, but relaxes the prior explicit no-work-for-never-strict lifecycle guard. Alternatively I will finalize blocked rather than claim the any-strict-config union contract. Which scope should I follow?
:RUN_ID:       run-01M5ZV61MEYAMQEGWE3N8CTWYV
:ASK_ID:       ask-7aedb36fba154f329ff2ebcc9ef21bd3
:TIMEOUT_SECS: 1800
:EVENT_ID:     b4eac202-0d19-47ff-8cb7-e9e4096c249b
:END:

* TX 2026-10-28 Wed 00:50:32 run.answered TASK-EW3VV
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261028-theaspirationalagent-6f74fbf5-341a-4c99-b884-6337f768b8db
:TIME:         [2026-10-28 Wed 00:50:32]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-EW3VV
:REASON:       Manager answer: runtime enabling must be covered (union adoption required; do not finalize blocked). Preferred mechanism, if it works in your code: create the accepted set lazily at the first strict dial, keyed by the configuration object (e.g. a module-level WeakMap in protocol-family.ts that the strict dial writes), and compute the union at the sink boundary as: configurations currently held by the framework that have an accepted set. Removal/stop/final close deletes that object's set only if one exists (keeps the existence-guard; nothing for never-strict configs). That needs no bookkeeping at registration. If that does not work (e.g. the dial cannot see the original object, or held-ness cannot be read at the sink), your proposal is acceptable as the fallback: an inert ownership record at registration with no wrapping, listeners, copies or output changes for legacy, accepted set only on strict dial, deleted on removal/stop/final close. Either way: add the non-strict-registered -> flipped-to-strict -> real dial test on both engines, B/C byte-identity for never-strict sets, same three files, under 300 net lines; report which mechanism you used and why. Quote back in one sentence.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M5ZV61MEYAMQEGWE3N8CTWYV
:ASK_ID:       ask-7aedb36fba154f329ff2ebcc9ef21bd3
:EVENT_ID:     d6d7419d-da66-4885-87e6-6d8bad80def3
:END:

* TX 2026-10-28 Wed 00:51:15 run.message TASK-EW3VV
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261028-theaspirationalagent-caeabe07-8ad2-4272-a73b-f4410709ac6f
:TIME:         [2026-10-28 Wed 00:51:15]
:TYPE:         run.message
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-EW3VV
:REASON:       Additions to my answer on ask-7aedb36f (quote back in one sentence): (1) A WeakMap is not iterable: build the union per framework by walking THAT framework's held configurations and looking each up. Define 'held' in the report: a stopped/removed/finally-closed configuration contributes nothing even if it is still in the config list; a reconnect-pending one keeps contributing. (2) Flip-to-strict covers only credentials accepted by strict dials; credentials a legacy dial accepted before the flip are not redacted. State this in README and report. (3) Flip back to false while still held: the rule is that the existing accepted set stays and keeps redacting until removal/stop/final close (consistent with the existence-guard). Test that on both engines. (4) Same object in two framework instances (or added twice): a framework must never lose redaction for a credential its own strict dial accepted because another instance removed/stopped the object. Over-redaction across instances (B redacts a credential accepted via A for a shared object) is acceptable if documented; under-redaction is not. Pick a design that guarantees this (e.g. per-framework ownership or reference counting), add a two-instance control, and state the behaviour in the report.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M5ZV61MEYAMQEGWE3N8CTWYV
:MESSAGE_ID:   msg-3f0e9895e4e7467698283375672d5ca2
:FROM:         member:tarn2
:DELIVERY:     queued
:EVENT_ID:     657cb2d5-a1ef-4d44-8add-6ef9b7d4a303
:END:

Treat the 366-pass figure as unreproducible historical evidence and say so in the report. Write replacement tests instead, red-first (red on layer 2 without layer 3 where they concern recipients; red on layer 1 without #361 where they concern the hold): (a) the liveness witness from qualification-summary.json: an overlapping wake during a budget hold produces exactly one subsequent reply and does not stall, while a fresh-after-summary control passes; (b) independent thread roots stay independent through holds/retries/tool waits; (c) the frozen recipient envelope survives queue/tool/retry/budget restart and isn't replaced by a later context-only arrival; (d) explicit-send destination honoured; (e) stale typing stop doesn't clear a successor's activity; (f) partial/other-recipient handshake promise refused. Use only synthetic fixtures. Do not go looking in agent homes or state.

:REASON:       W §10 leaves timeout bounds, maximum pending count, overload refusal, and connection fairness unresolved before implementation. For slice A, may I use a required timeoutMs integer in 1..60000, at most 64 pending preflights per connection (including attestation/validation), immediate admission_denied on overload, and independent per-connection budgets with sequential channel-batch items; or what exact limits/refusal/fairness should I implement?
:RUN_ID:       run-01M63VVZ46SV58RQ6QHPHXKTXN
:ASK_ID:       ask-3bd8edd0f3424c14ad472650212097ad

* TX 2026-10-29 Thu 12:59:59 run.answered TASK-HVQ8K
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261029-theaspirationalagent-f93cdf95-3d2e-4fc6-8b42-d4aff56f1057
:TIME:         [2026-10-29 Thu 12:59:59]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HVQ8K
:REASON:       Manager answer (Tarn's engineering call, not an owner decision; record it as such): yes, with these exact choices. timeoutMs is a required integer in 1..60000 on the required-admission config (validated at config load; invalid config refuses to start the connection, it does not default). At most 64 pending preflights per connection, counting attestation/validation; the 65th is refused immediately with admission_denied (no queueing, no wait). Budgets are independent per connection; items of one channel batch are admitted sequentially in batch order, each with its own timeout. Make 60000 and 64 named constants, document them as initial bounds open to maintainer review, and test: timeout -> nothing admitted; overload refusal; one connection's saturation does not affect another; batch order preserved.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M63VVZ46SV58RQ6QHPHXKTXN
:ASK_ID:       ask-3bd8edd0f3424c14ad472650212097ad
:EVENT_ID:     aedea61d-0d19-4ab2-b2a9-4d697c01b6c1
:END:

For A I am relying on the existing MCPL decoder/grant implementation at pinned framework U, adding no wire vocabulary. Is that sufficient for A, or please provide the MCPL SHA/repository path that must be read/recorded?

* TX 2026-10-29 Thu 13:21:07 run.answered TASK-HVQ8K
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261029-theaspirationalagent-cd5efaf2-d875-4655-a2ae-4bbc58d00399
:TIME:         [2026-10-29 Thu 13:21:07]
:TYPE:         run.answered

:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework

:REASON:       Manager answer (Tarn's engineering call): that's sufficient for A. Rely on the MCPL decoder and grant implementation already inside upstream framework 4a45766f019fa11c8699645bd6806ebf3e13e5d1 (its vendored or dependency mcpl-core version, which you record in the report from package-lock/package.json). Add no wire vocabulary. In the report and the PR-facing text, state that slice A doesn't depend on any SPEC change and that the full MCPL pin is deferred to slice E. No external MCPL repo read is required.

* TX 2026-10-29 Thu 13:32:02 run.asked TASK-HVQ8K
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261029-theaspirationalagent-e0108e74-17da-473a-a641-870c76ee56c0
:TIME:         [2026-10-29 Thu 13:32:02]
:TYPE:         run.asked
:ACTOR:        orgasmic
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HVQ8K
:REASON:       One ordering detail: the current required transport guard refuses hinted pushes after the pushEvents grant, but before PushHandler can validate the enabled feature set. W's lane matrix says existing authorization stays first, including feature authorization. I intend to keep the early tap unconditionally suppressed for required connections and move the hints refusal into the required push wrapper immediately after pure feature validation, still before the hook/dedup/notices. Is that the intended ordering (so unknown feature + hints yields the existing feature error, not admission_unsupported)?
:RUN_ID:       run-01M63VVZ46SV58RQ6QHPHXKTXN
:ASK_ID:       ask-3d4b2cd1ae8d489388ac43650d478ca1
:TIMEOUT_SECS: 1800
:EVENT_ID:     5b0f4c79-652b-4647-bb75-a4d65b05f83d
:END:

* TX 2026-10-29 Thu 13:32:10 run.answered TASK-HVQ8K
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261029-theaspirationalagent-d59ccbfe-7133-4581-8fc5-75d1c59aeefb
:TIME:         [2026-10-29 Thu 13:32:10]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HVQ8K
:REASON:       Manager answer (Tarn's engineering call): yes, that's the intended ordering. Existing authorization comes first, including grant and feature-set validation. For required connections the early tap stays suppressed unconditionally. The hints refusal goes in the required push wrapper right after pure feature validation, before the hook, dedup and notices. So unknown feature + hints gives the existing feature error, and a valid feature + hints gives the admission refusal. Add a test for each of those two cases, and one showing that a non-required connection with hints is unchanged.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M63VVZ46SV58RQ6QHPHXKTXN
:ASK_ID:       ask-3d4b2cd1ae8d489388ac43650d478ca1
:MESSAGE_ID:   msg-848744e3b52148b19e43b69bb00dd4d7
:DELIVERY:     delivered
:EVENT_ID:     2f957331-95f5-4b9f-8e52-c370c6e3fe25
:END:

:REASON:       O1 boundary: cached/installed context-manager 0.24.1 (integrity pinned in persistence-preflight.txt) preserves metadata at src/message-store.ts:686-720,2347 and exposes summary lineage via types/strategy.ts:2430-2438 plus context-manager.ts:2449-2452, so no companion is presently needed for provenance. But its open path (context-manager.ts:518-545,568-580), Chronicle 0.5.1 open config (index.d.ts:89-101,276), and base framework.ts:450-455 have no minimum-reader/classification compatibility gate: rolling back to the base will ignore a new stamp and classify it as conversational. D can add fail-closed startup when this NEW reader has the profile disabled, but cannot make an unchanged old reader refuse metadata-only stamps. Is that disabled-profile test plus an explicit prohibition on old-code rollback the intended O1 acceptance, or must D remain storage-disabled pending a companion minimum-reader gate/backport? No production edits yet; the base gate is running.

:REASON:       Yes for this slice: (1) the NEW reader fails closed at startup when stamped rows exist and the profile is disabled/ignored (test it), (2) unknown/malformed/future stamps never promoted, (3) the storage profile stays default-off and is never offered without explicit enablement, and (4) docs + changelog + PR body state plainly that enabling the profile is one-way: rolling back to framework code older than this change is unsupported and would treat stamped rows as conversational, because the current context-manager/Chronicle open path has no minimum-reader gate. Put the missing minimum-reader/classification gate in context-manager (with your file:line evidence) as an explicit maintainer question and follow-up, not a change here. Do not touch context-manager. :TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 09:38:28]
:TYPE:         run.asked
:ACTOR:        orgasmic
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-XHZ4H
:REASON:       One wire-boundary clarification before edits: section 1 says push behavior is unchanged except reserved-key collision handling, while section 3/design M3 forbids silently downgrading an unsupported push selector and section 4 requires a mutation for push accepting the field. Should a top-level delivery field on push/event be explicitly rejected before effects (unsupported_combination, leaving no-field push unchanged), or remain an ignored unknown field as on the base? I will not enable context-only effects on push either way.

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 09:39:42 run.answered TASK-XHZ4H
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-a11b3ca8-ed0a-4ee7-98f8-92edde886d75
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 09:39:42]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-XHZ4H
:REASON:       Explicitly reject. A push/event carrying a top-level `delivery` field is refused before any effect with the closed reason `unsupported_combination` (no new reason), whatever its value; never ignored, never downgraded to normal. Push without the field stays byte-identical to the base (covered by the no-config differential corpus). Gate it only on presence of the field, so it does not depend on grants or the storage profile. Document it as the second explicit wire delta next to the reserved-key collision delta, in docs, changelog and PR body, and keep the mutation `push accepts field`. If push has no per-message result channel at your base, refuse the way push already refuses invalid events and say which path that is with file:line. Quote this answer in the report. — Tarn
:RUN_ID:       run-01M6629SJZ1Q0E6H5WVBAX4E6B
:ASK_ID:       ask-88034f6e43604c81aed5a5b19797a90e

:END:

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 10:41:03 run.answered TASK-HVQ8K
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-744816db-d378-4cb4-b6c8-59aa238fe5b8
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 10:41:03]
:TYPE:         run.answered

:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HVQ8K
:REASON:       Confirmed. Limit the cap to mayWake:false, so on a required connection with mayWake:true (or absent) a gate decline behaves exactly as on an unconfigured connection, where modules can still wake. Replace the two old tests' intent: (1) with mayWake:true a module may wake despite a false gate, and the module-visible gate decision is preserved; (2) KEEP a test that mayWake:false still caps every downstream wake, module requestInference included, and that a later policy veto (an actual mayWake:false) still downgrades the module-visible event. Rename the tests to match. Docs/changelog: mayWake:false caps downstream wakes; mayWake:true does not override gate declines. Cite R1-10 in the commit and in the disposition.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M6669APQD2QBQ44ZZ6DRVMCT
:ASK_ID:       ask-98d018a780354e77bb1fc26d5abe1ef7
:EVENT_ID:     798f4ed8-be56-41f1-956d-f973a2a46493

Tests over real stdio: both lanes × mayWake false/true. mayWake:false still never wakes. mayWake:true wakes and its automatic reply is bound to the triggering work. A module wake with no admitted event remains unbound and its reply is refused as before. Make it a separate commit titled as a rebase interaction fix, and list it in the report and in the PR body.

:REASON:       Don't add new token-release semantics. Mirror the existing drop path at ~19617 exactly; that is the review's fix direction, and upstream's own branch behaves the same way. Change the tests to assert what endContinuedTurn actually does in the stale branch: a turn-end/dropped trace is emitted, the logical turn and its routing are cleared, the tool-result guard is abandoned, a waiting ephemeral run settles, and deferred messages flush. Assert that the next wake is no longer requeued forever, if the scaffold allows it (with no frame present). :PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-MD5AH
:REASON:       The real MCPL regression exposed one lifecycle ordering issue: normal completion calls gate.onInferenceEnded before awaiting final channel publication, so a held batch is checked before publishedAfterReading is recorded and still wakes. May I remove that early normal-completion gate release and rely on the existing unconditional finally release after speech dispatch? Agent.reset and inference:completed timing stay unchanged; failures/aborts keep their existing release paths. This holds only gate debounce delivery until the final reply actually settles.

:REASON:       Call-site audit found queueCoalescedWakes also admits render-batch wakes. Before materialization it has no placement; its plain deferred-render branch has a deferredId/owner, but reply requirements depend on allAmbient for the entire render, not the latest event tags GateEventInfo carries. I propose leaving this aggregate-render path unmarked (as an explicit conservative limitation), rather than changing gate tag semantics or expanding the placement API again. Ordinary module, channel, push, and message-mode heartbeat placements are wired. Is that scoped exception acceptable?

:REASON:       For B-M1/B-M2, may I add optional EventGate getSharedSlotReaders() host integration and use this trade-off: write one shared summary containing only events still unseen by every shared-slot reader, omit divergent placement-backed events from that summary (their original content remains stored), but retain each recipient’s own unseen-event wake/provenance; isolated fork recipients receive their own summaries? * TX 2026-10-30 Fri 19:52:11 run.asked TASK-HJV3J
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-6cc7ac39-5ca9-4db3-a1f9-088e3a519bc3
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 19:52:11]
:TYPE:         run.asked
:ACTOR:        orgasmic
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HJV3J
:REASON:       Two API choices need confirmation for narrowed B: (1) Does dropping experimental.replyRouting mean remove nested threadId/threaded transport entirely and represent every approved destination with the existing registered ChannelRef (including registered thread channels), also removing initializeExtensions if unused? (2) For host approval re-check at send, may I add a required synchronous replyRouting.authorize(input, destination) callback, called at binding and immediately before each automatic open/send, while resolve remains bounded async; null, malformed, throw, timeout or unavailable route would keep admission/wake unchanged and carry private no-route authority with an agent notice? This separates route failure from A admission refusal.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M675WMW5VJZFQ9S6N1351R2R
:ASK_ID:       ask-1f5c9fdc2d7e4ccb8b26cde49c9a25c1
:TIMEOUT_SECS: 1800
:EVENT_ID:     f0ccb6b9-bbe6-4720-ac67-fa02b2bdd2f1
:END:

:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-7b52819f-9634-4198-89e8-776939fe7b29

:TASK:         TASK-HJV3J
:REASON:       (1) Yes. Remove the nested threadId/threaded-transport additions and the replyRouting profile entirely. Represent every approved destination with the existing registered ChannelRef (a registered thread channel is just a ChannelRef). Remove initializeExtensions too if nothing else uses it after that; if something else does, keep it and say why in the report. A thread target contract is a separate later proposal, not in this PR.

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 19:53:35 run.asked TASK-HJV3J
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-b18d2744-a509-4468-aead-257f927b5d9e
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 19:53:35]
:TYPE:         run.asked
:ACTOR:        orgasmic
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-HJV3J
:REASON:       To keep routing timeout separate from A's admission refusal, I propose moving B's optional consumer outside RequiredAdmission.run: first complete A's unchanged bounded admission; then run the optional consumer with a separate bounded timeoutMs (required on replyAuthority, 1..60000), its own abort controller, and cancellation on signal/connection invalidation. Route errors return private work only; A's final current/check still controls stale admission. A's pending slot is released at its existing verdict boundary, and the routing consumer caps its own pending resolutions at 64 (overload => private, never admission_denied). This replaces round 3's same-deadline post-verdict seam. Is that separation approved?
:RUN_ID:       run-01M675WMW5VJZFQ9S6N1351R2R
:ASK_ID:       ask-805f26737e894f34b79120406a6db2b3
:TIMEOUT_SECS: 1800
:EVENT_ID:     f895c4a2-3366-4b26-a486-700d913cee95
:END:

:REASON:       An addition to M-A (please confirm in the report): the modern-family exclusion only restores config compatibility. It must NOT grant context-only delivery. Add negative controls: a modern server with `['channels.*']` is accepted, yet it still gets no context-only grant or mode. Context-only still requires an MCPL-family advertised and granted `channels.contextOnly` plus a validated mode, with no fallback wake and no unsupported-enable path.

:REASON:       LOW-b identity boundary: ChannelEntry object identity exists, but handleChanged(serverId, params) and removeServer(serverId) receive only an id; an already-late invocation cannot identify which registration it intended. May I thread the existing connection identity from framework handlers into registry lifecycle calls (optional host-only expected connection, guarded against the current registry connection), with entry-identity guarded retirement? Otherwise I will mark the stale-invocation portion BLOCKED-FOR-SCOPE rather than broad-delete by key.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M67JH84WHFD8T7T4938H7ZWM

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 23:35:36 run.answered TASK-PK8RC
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-c62a614a-ae70-4bc7-a762-df23dc03ea9d
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 23:35:36]
:TYPE:         run.answered
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-PK8RC
:REASON:       Yes (call by the task operator Tarn within Soma's boundary "use the existing connection/registration identity"; not an owner decision of Vasyl's). Conditions: (1) thread only the EXISTING connection identity from the framework handlers into the registry lifecycle calls as an optional, internal (host-only) expected-connection parameter; don't change the public export/signature of exported functions in an incompatible way, and don't introduce a new identity type; (2) delete the hint only when BOTH match: the expected connection == the registry's current connection for that server AND the ChannelEntry object being retired == the entry that was registered (entry-identity guarded); (3) if the expected identity is absent (an external host/old caller) or doesn't match → DON'T delete the hint (conservative = current behaviour), with no broad delete by key; record that case as a remaining gap in docs/report; (4) admission ordering, epoch, held dispatch, grants, and the inbound-admission.ts and RequiredAdmission blocks unchanged (byte-identical to A/B); (5) controls: remove→re-register; stale-old-teardown→new-hint (late call with the old identity → the new hint survives); disconnect-with-registration-kept; absent-identity → no delete; plus mutations: dropping the connection check and dropping the entry check are each killed. If you find the existing identity doesn't distinguish replacement in some path, mark that path BLOCKED-FOR-SCOPE and carry on with the rest.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M67JH84WHFD8T7T4938H7ZWM
:ASK_ID:       ask-d8e3a4466aad44398ba60d58fecd0c99
:EVENT_ID:     c15cddea-d626-4659-84b2-a6bfa01a1b9f

* TX 2026-10-30 Fri 23:36:48 run.message TASK-PK8RC
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261030-theaspirationalagent-e3d287a8-b87b-46f8-98b3-e3ab6b98d9c7
:TIME:         [2026-10-30 Fri 23:36:48]
:TYPE:         run.message
:ACTOR:        tarn2
:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-PK8RC
:REASON:       Binding additions to ask-d8e3 (Soma, programme qualifier; within scope, no owner decision): (1) identity is TRANSPORT-OWNED (the connection object/epoch held by the transport), not a claimed server id/metadata. The expected old registration is CAPTURED from the relevant lifecycle event (at registration/close of that connection), not looked up as "current" at deletion time and compared to itself. (2) The same connection object can span reconnects: tests for stale teardown AFTER replacement AND for a same-object reconnect/epoch change. Use the existing epoch/entry identity if it exists; if it can't be distinguished → an explicit BLOCKED gap, not a claim of generation safety. (3) On mismatch in the identity-guarded path, don't delete the replacement's ChannelEntry either (not just spare its hint): test this. :REASON:       Binding additions (Soma, programme qualifier; passed on by the task operator Tarn). (1) Per-item capture WITHOUT refreshing away transport provenance: removing one registration doesn't refuse a DIFFERENT current one, but items with the SAME invalidated registration still refuse. (2) A transport/connection-epoch change invalidates ALL old-epoch batch items; DON'T read the new epoch per item. The epoch is captured once for the batch from the transport event; only the registration is captured per item. (3) Tests in both error directions: m2 can't borrow m1's valid scope to admit an unknown/unreadable registration; the receipt/messageId association stays exact (each result = its own item). (4) No lazy-register/fallback in the opt-in guarded path. (5) A/B/grant/verdict and profile-off unchanged; the first failed differential stays separate evidence. Tests: same-registration-stale (both refused), different-registration-stale (only that one), an epoch change mid-batch (all old ones refused), a borrowing attempt, the messageId mapping. Mutants: a per-item epoch read, sharing a ref across items, a lazy fallback, each killed.

:REASON:       The exact-stdout assertion plus JSON evidence checks passes pristine tests and kills evidence deletion/hash removal, but the specified late-hash reorder mutant still survives on both Nodes: framework diagnostics are on stderr, so stdout remains exactly the evidence line. May I add one explicit test-only FORK_SEED_WITNESS_STARTED stdout marker immediately after the hash assertion and before any witness work? The mandated exact-stdout assertion would then deterministically kill late validation without touching src/deps/production guard. I will retain the first survivor observations and rerun the specified mutants and fresh head cells only after the decision.

* TX 2026-10-31 Sat 03:30:51 run.answered TASK-XHZ4H
:PROPERTIES:
:TX_ID:        tx-20261031-theaspirationalagent-138d7cef-2608-4eb6-b63d-56541de49061
:TIME:         [2026-10-31 Sat 03:30:51]
:TYPE:         run.answered

:MACHINE:      c17b4726-2197-4476-a265-4515fb584352
:PROJECT:      theaspirational.agent-framework
:TASK:         TASK-XHZ4H
:REASON:       Yes, this is the task operator Tarn's call (not the owner's): a test-only marker FORK_SEED_WITNESS_STARTED is allowed, under these conditions: (1) it's a SEPARATE first statement of the witness work (before framework creation/addMessage/fork), not bundled with the hash assert, so that moving the hash assert lower leaves the marker in front of the witness work; (2) the mismatch test: stdout === exactly the evidence line (the marker is absent); (3) the positive test: the evidence line, then the marker, then FORK_SEED_ALIGNMENT_OK, in that order; (4) an extra mutant: move the marker below the witness work → must be killed by the positive test (otherwise the marker doesn't prove "before"); (5) the first survivor observations of the reorder mutant (both Nodes) stay in the report as the first-try result, not erased; (6) no src/deps/production guard changes. Then all mutants (reorder, evidence deletion, hash removal, marker-move) and the fresh head cells as in the brief.
:RUN_ID:       run-01M67ZSW69W73G44KPCTCACQ7Z

:REASON:       A clarification of LOW-2 (Soma's condition): don't choose a rename — the outer+item checks must compose BY CONSTRUCTION on the usable path: the publicly reachable fork (what a batch lane calls) must already include the outer check, so a caller can't skip it; a safer name/comment alone doesn't count. A test: a direct call to the reachable fork without the helper still refuses on outer framework.ts:35200-35203 conditions (queue closed → admission_not_ready; foreign connection → admission_stale) + a mutant that removes the composition → killed. The replacement behaviour: explicit verdict controls for both cases (the replacement's scope resolved → m2 accepted against the new generation; unresolved → the expected verdict per the current code, and if it's a false pass — STOP/ask). Keep the explanation of the npm-ls resolution context (the symlinked .matched-base) and the earlier failed evidence (the first differential, the dev failure) in the report.

DON'T redefine non-configurable native methods and DON'T patch SDK/loader exports (beyond the existing mock.module for stripUnpairedToolBlocks) — remember the "Cannot redefine property" lesson.
